**V. 21. ***{And Joram said, Make ready}* Heb. *Bind.* See 1 King. 18. 44. Certainly had he yet feared any enemy, he would not have exposed his person to such danger. But it was God's work to harden his heart.

*{And his chariot was made ready}* His subjects and his servants readily yielded to what he commanded.

*{And Joram king of Israel and Ahaziah king of Judah went out}* This also was of God, that Ahaziah should go with him, 2 Chr. 22. 7.

*{each in his chariot}* For greater state and better help one to another, if need were.

*{and they went out against Jehu}* Not with a purpose to fight, but to meet him, and know the end of his coming.

*{and met him}* Heb. *found him.* God so brought the matter to pass as if he had gone out to seek him in such a place, 1 King. 21. 20.

*{in the portion of Naboth the Jezreelite}* See v. 10. This place might have put him in mind of the judgment threatened, 1 King. 21. 19.

**V. 22. ***{And it came to pass, when Joram saw Jehu}* As yet he fears no evil from that troop, and thereupon meets Jehu in the face.

*{that he said, Is it peace, Jehu?}* Is all well at Ramoth? Have you gotten any further advantage against the Syrians? Or have they gotten advantage against you? Why do you thus come from Ramoth with your troops?

*{And he answered}* Jehu now discovering the advantage he had gotten, makes a quick and peremptory answer.

*{What peace?}* The interrogative implies a strong negation. You may not expect any peace, being so wicked as you are.

*{so long as the whoredoms}* Whoredoms properly set forth bodily pollutions of man with woman; metaphorically, idolatry: For God is a husband to his people, and they his spouse. When they therefore worship idols, they forsake their true husband, and prostitute themselves to adulterers. In this spiritual acception the word is oft used, as Lev. 17. 7. Judg. 2. 17. Jer. 3. 2. In both senses it may here be taken: For Jezebel was a notorious adulteress and idolatress.

*{of thy mother Jezebel}* The sins of his mother are laid to his charge, because he suffered her, and dealt not with her as Asa did with his mother, 1 King. 15. 13. Yea he continued in her idolatry; if he were not also an adulterer.

*{and her witchcrafts}* Witchcraft is commonly used for a cunning device to deceive, Nah. 3. 4. All idolaters in this respect use witchcrafts: and they are therefore joined together here. It may be also that Jezebel wrought with the devil, and was a plain witch. Certain it is she bewitched many with her cunning, as the elders, nobles, and others in Jezreel, 1 King. 21. 8, etc.

*{are so many}* They who give themselves over to wickedness, set no bounds nor measure thereunto.

**V. 23. ***{And Joram turned his hands}* He spoke to his chariot-man to turn his horses. See 1 King. 22. 34.

*{and fled}* For he was afraid, having no power to resist such an army as came against him.

*{and said to Ahaziah}* Who was not far from him, and whom he conceived to be in danger as well as himself.

*{There is treachery, O Ahaziah}* Jehu and they that came with him were Joram's subjects: and for subjects to rise against their sovereign is treachery and plain treason, if at least they have not a warrant from God, as Jehu here had, v. 6, etc.

**V. 24. ***{And Jehu drew a bow with his full strength}* Heb. *filled his hand with a bow.* In Scripture they that take a thing into their hands are said *to fill their hands therewith.* See 1 King. 13. 33. Here the phrase may imply that strength which Jehu put forth in drawing his bow.

*{and smote Jehoram between his arms, and the arrow went out at his heart}* He was not shot as he met Jehu face to face, but as he fled from him; so as the arrow pierced through his back-part into his heart.

*{and he sunk down}* Heb. *bowed.* He instantly died. He was not smitten here as he was by the Syrians, ch. 8. 28. or as Ahazia was smitten, v. 27. but he was slain downright.

*{in his chariot}* For he endeavored to fly away in it, v. 21, 23.

**V. 25. ***{Then said Jehu to Bidkar his Captain}* Bidkar before was a fellow commander with Jehu under Ahab and Joram; but now he was under Jehu, being one of those that proclaimed him king, v. 13.

*{Take up and cast him}* Jehu would not suffer Joram's servants to carry him back into the city, but would have him pulled down out of the chariot, and cast upon the open ground.

*{in the portion of the field of Naboth the Jezreelite}* See v. 10.

*{for remember, how that when I and thou rode together after Ahab his father}* Being under Ahab, commanders in his army, in that respect they followed him as attendants, and so had the fitter opportunity to see what he did, and to hear what he spoke, or what was spoken to him.

*{the Lord laid this burden upon him}* Threatenings of prophets are usually called *burdens*, Isa. 13. 1. Mal. 1. 1. For as burdens lie heavy upon the shoulders of a man, so divine judgments, both threatened and executed, lie heavy upon the soul of a man. This burden is said to be laid on Ahab when the judgment was first threatened against him, 1 King. 21. 24. and it is now executed.

**V. 26. ***{Surely I have seen}* Heb. *If I have not seen, etc.* This is a form of an oath. Something is to be understood to make up the sense, as thus, *If I have not seen, let me not be accounted a God.* This form imports the certainty of a thing, and is to the sense well translated by our English, *Surely I have seen.* It is like the doubling of the phrase Exo. 3. 7. *I have seen, I have seen*, i.e., I have surely seen.

*{yesterday}* This has relation to 1 King. 21. 19. For Naboth being slain in the evening, Ahab went the next day to take possession of his vineyard; and then Elijah met him there, and denounced this judgment against him. *Yesterday* is oft put for the time past. See ch. 13. 5.

*{the blood}* Heb. *bloods.* For all his blood out of every part of his body was shed; and it was after such a manner as if the blood of many had been shed.

*{of Naboth, and the blood of his sons}* This shows that Naboth's sons were killed as well as himself. See 1 King. 21. 13.

*{said the Lord}* By the ministry of Elijah. Though this be not expressed in the history, yet might Jehu hear it uttered.

*{and I will requite thee in this plat}* Or *portion.* Namely where Naboth's vineyard was. See v. 10.

*{saith the Lord}* The Lord sees the sins of the wicked, to take vengeance of them in his time.

*{now therefore take and cast him into the plat of ground, according to the word of the Lord}* See v. 25. 1 King. 21. 21. Jehu in that which he did against Ahab's house and against Baal, had an eye to the word of God; but not in other things. See ch. 30. 31.

**V. 27. ***{But when Ahaziah the King of Judah saw this}* Namely that which befell Joram.

*{he fled by the way of the garden-house}* He dared not turn back in that open way that led to Jezreel, but fled by a byway without the suburbs, where their gardens were.

*{and Jehu followed after him}* Jehu had a watchful eye over the two kings; and having slain one upon the place, he eagerly pursues the other.

*{and said, Smite him also in the chariot}* There were some of his soldiers nearer to Ahaziah than himself was; to them he speaks, and bids them slay Ahaziah, even while he was in his chariot, i.e., speedily, not giving him any time to light out.

*{and they did so at the going up to Gur}* Gur was in the way to Samaria.

*{which is by Ibleam}* This belonged to Manasseh, Josh. 17. 11. as did Megiddo also.

*{and he fled to Megiddo}* See 1 King. 9. 15. Here Josiah received his deadly wound, ch. 23. 29.

*{and died there}* He was not slain outright in his chariot, as Joram was, v. 14. For he was wounded in one place, and died in another. Whereas it is said 2 Chron. 22. 9. that *he was hid in Samaria*, it is thus to be taken, that seeking to hide himself, there he was discovered and brought to Jehu, who thereupon commanded him to be smitten; upon which smiting, the stroke being mortal, he fled to Megiddo, and there died. Or *Samaria* may be indefinitely taken for the whole country, as 1 King. 18. 2. and then Megiddo may be comprised under *Samaria.*

**V. 28. ***{And his servants carried him in a chariot to Jerusalem}* After he was slain Jehu looked no more after him, but suffered his servants to carry him whither they would. Or out of respect to Jehoshaphat his grandfather, as 2 Chr. 22. 9. he might suffer him to be carried to Jerusalem.

*{and buried him in his sepulchre with his fathers}* The Jews counted it an honor to be buried by their fathers.

*{in the city of David}* See 1 King. 2. 10.

**V. 29. ***{and in the eleventh year, &c.}* See ch. 8. 25.

**V. 30. ***{And when Jehu was come to Jezreel}* Two kings being slain by Jehu, none dared oppose his entrance into the city, but he quickly took possession thereof.

*{Jezebel heard of it}* Ill news flies fast; and such famous matters as the death of two kings could not but be quickly spread abroad far and near, and so come to her ears.

*{and she painted her face}* Heb. *put her eyes in painting.* She had painted her face all over, so as her eyes were in the midst of painting. She could not be but well grown in years at this time, and in that respect this youthful and lustful setting forth herself was the more detestable.

*{and tired her head}* She set herself out in the greatest bravery she could. This argued a very strange disposition. The accidents newly fallen out should rather have moved her to have rent her clothes, and torn her hair from her head, and slubbered her face with tears: but herein she showed her great pride and stoutness, as if she would daunt Jehu by showing that she would yet still retain her royal state, and not care or fear what he could do to her. Some will have it, that she so attired herself to entice Jehu to lust, or at least to induce others to aid her, Jer. 4. 30. Ezek. 23. 40.

*{and looked out at a window}* To outbrave him to his face.

**V. 31. ***{And as Jehu entered in at the gate}* Either the king's palace was at the city gate, where seats of judgment used to be set; or here is meant the gate of the king's palace, which was within the city. At this time was Jehu in his greatest pomp and triumph; which further sets out Jezebel's resolute and undaunted spirit.

*{she said, Had Zimri peace}* She resembles Jehu to Zimri, and puts him in mind of Zimri's end; *q.d.* you well know that Zimri did not long prosper, but came to a quick, fearful and utter destruction. See 1 Kings 16. 9, etc.

*{who slew his master}* She makes Jehu like Zimri, in that both of them killed their king. As Zimri was a commander in his king's army, 1 King. 16. 9. so was Jehu in his, v. 11. therefore both the one and the other's sovereign is called his *master.* But though both of them therein agreed, yet they did what they did upon a far different ground. Jehu had such a warrant v. 7. as Zimri had not; whereupon he needed not to fear the like issue.

**V. 32. ***{And he lift up his face to the window}* He heard her voice, and was moved with her daring upbraiding speech; and therefore looks up to the place where she was, to see if he might take present vengeance on her.

*{and said, Who is on my side?}* Who will show that he takes part with me, and acknowledge me to be king?

*{who}* This doubling of the word implies confidence that some would do as he would have them; and withal, an earnest desire that they should show themselves.

*{and there looked out to him two or three}* God wonderfully wrought on their hearts, that they should so suddenly yield to another king, and that in the presence of the queen their mistress.

*{Eunuchs}* Or, *chamberlains*, to wit, of the queen, or other great officers attending upon her. See 1 King. 22. 9. Certainly they thought some vengeance would fall upon their queen; and thereupon, though they had a special relation to her, yet by looking out they give an intimation to Jehu that they were ready to do what he should command.

**V. 33. ***{And he said, Throw her down}* Jehu knew she had been the cause of much blood, besides her idolatry; and therefore requires that she should be tumbled down from that high room wherein she was.

*{So they threw her down}* Though it was a hard charge given to them, she being their mistress, yet having no hope of further advantage by attending on her, and withal being afraid of Jehu, they quickly execute it.

*{and some of her blood was sprinkled on the wall, and on the horses}* It seems her fall was from a high place, in that therewith her blood was so sprinkled all about. Or it may be the sprinkling of her blood was by the treading of the horses upon her.

*{and he trod her under foot}* Jehu with his troops being on horseback with their horses trampled upon her.

**V. 34. ***{And when he was come in, he did eat and drink}* Having taken much pains in his expedition between Ramoth and Jezreel, and having destroyed all that dared oppose him, when he was come into the city, and saw all quiet before him, he refreshed himself with meat and drink.

*{and said, Go see now}* His mind was not so set upon his repast as to forget what he had done. But being mindful of what befell Jezebel, he sends to see her dead body.

*{this cursed woman}* She was cursed in her former disposition, and now also in her present condition.

*{and bury her}* Burial was in those days counted an honor and a blessing. Wherefore though he had justly taken away her life, yet he would afford her that honor. See on Eccl. 6. 3.

*{for she is a Kings daughter}* Of the king of Zidon, 1 King. 16. 31. Though Jehu had upon divine warrant slain the two kings of Israel and Judah, yet being not willing to incense the king of Zidon against him, he testifies some respect to him.

**V. 35. ***{And they went to bury her}* Neither Jehu nor they whom he sent at this time remembered that which Elijah had foretold concerning Jezebel, 1 King. 21. 23. otherwise neither he nor they would have thought of burying her.

*{but they found no more of her then the skull, and the feet, and the palms of her hands}* All these were hard bones; and under these questionless the thighbones, and other hard bones of the body are comprised. As for all her flesh, the dogs had eaten it. She herself was of a doggish disposition, and in her lifetime did fly upon and tear the servants of God; and thereupon is she justly torn in pieces and devoured by dogs.

**V. 36. ***{Wherefore they came again, and told him}* What they had found of her, and what had been eaten of her.

*{and he said, This is the word of the Lord}* Evidences of such things as God has foretold do bring to mind and memory such things as had been forgotten.

*{which he spake by his servant}* Heb. *by the hand of his servant.* See 1 King. 8. 53.

*{Elijah the Tishbite}* See 1 King. 17. 1.

*{saying, In the portion of Jezreel}* See v. 10.

*{shall dogs eat the flesh of Jezebel}* See 1 King. 21. 23.

**V. 37. ***{And the carcase of Jezebel}* So much of her body as was left by the dogs.

*{shall be as dung upon the face of the field}* Shall be contemptible, trodden underfoot, and lie to rot in the open air.

*{in the portion of Jezreel: so that they shall not say, This is Jezebel}* When all the flesh is torn from the bones, who can know that such were the bones of such a party? This latter clause is not in the history, 1 King. 21. 23. but yet it being here set down, we are to account it as true as if it had been before expressed.

## CHAPTER 10

**Ver. 1. ***{And Ahab had seventy sons}* Under this word *sons* may grandsons also be comprised, Gen. 31. 43. Ahab himself and Joram his son might have many wives, and many children by them; yea Ahab might have more sons than are by name mentioned in Scripture, and they have also sons; and thus the number of sons amount to seventy. Great men of old were wont to have many children, Judg. 8. 30. & 10. 4. & 12. 9, 14. 2 Chr. 11. 21.

*{in Samaria}* This was the royal city, populous and well fortified: thereupon in those troublesome times the king's children and kindred were there.

*{and Jehu wrote letters}* As Naboth and his children were slain by letters from Ahab and Jezebel, 1 King. 21. 8. so their children are by letters caused to be slain.

*{and sent to Samaria}* He knew that all Ahab's progeny was there.

*{unto the rulers of Jezreel}* These might indeed be rulers of Jezreel, but yet now come to Samaria with the king's children for the greater safety.

*{to the Elders}* See 1 King. 8. 1.

*{and to them that brought up Ahabs children}* Heb. *nourishers.* Such as had the tuition of them, and were to educate them and take care of their safety.

**V. 2. ***{Now as soon as this letter cometh to you}* Jehu began his letter as the king of Syria began his, ch. 5. 6.

*{seeing your masters sons are with you}* These were not only the king's subjects, but also his officers and special servants, and in that respect he is called their *master.* See ch. 9. 7. What sons are here meant is showed v. 1.

*{and there are with you chariots and horses, &c.}* These were means of defending themselves against an enemy. And therefore to try whether they would stand against him, or yield unto him, he puts them in mind of the provision they had.

**V. 3. ***{Look even out the best and meetest of your masters sons}* It is probable some of the seventy were of age and prowess; so as still he seems to put them on to do the utmost they could.

*{and set him on his fathers throne}* He means they should both make him and proclaim him king; 1 King. 2. 24.

*{and fight for your Masters house}* For those that are remaining of Ahab's stock, or in any special manner belong to him. Jehu's meaning is not that indeed they should fight; but thus he tries their disposition the more thoroughly.

**V. 4. ***{But they were exceedingly afraid}* God that had appointed Jehu to be his sword to destroy the whole stock of Ahab, put a fear into their hearts, that notwithstanding the defense which they had they dared not rise up against him.

*{and said, Behold, two Kings stood not before him}* Jehoram and Ahaziah. See ch. 9. 21.

*{how then shall we stand?}* They might better have stood against Jehu than both those kings, who were not at that time in the midst of an army, and had an army coming furiously upon them, ch. 9. 20. but these were in a defensed city, and had store of ammunition; but fear possessed their hearts.

**V. 5. ***{And he that was over the house}* The chief of all those that appertained unto Ahab's house, or that had the command over the king's palace.

*{and he that was over the city}* The chief governor of Samaria.

*{the Elders also}* Such senators and counselors of state as there remained to order the affairs of that city, or the land. See 1 King. 8. 1.

*{and the bringers up of the children}* See v. 1.

*{sent to Jehu, saying}* They all sat together to consult and advise about that weighty message which Jehu had sent unto them, and returned with one consent the answer following.

*{We are thy servants}* At your command, acknowledging you our king.

*{and will do all that thou shalt bid us}* Fear makes them slavish, for they interpose no condition of just, honest, faithful, or any like qualification.

*{we will not make any King}* We will not set up any to oppose you, neither will we take part with any against you.

*{do thou that which is good in thine eyes}* Whatsoever pleases you.

**V. 6. ***{Then he wrote a letter the second time to them}* Like a prudent and subtle commander, he gives them no time to deliberate about that which he requires of them, but instantly makes known what he would have them do.

*{saying, If you be mine}* Heb. *for me.* To do as you have said.

*{and if you will hearken unto my voice}* Obey me, and do what I require.

*{take ye the heads}* Cut off the heads.

*{of the men your masters sons}* Those seventy mentioned v. 1.

*{and come to me to Jezreel}* There Jehu abode till he should know the mind of the governors in Samaria. He means that they should come with the heads of those sons.

*{by to morrow this time}* They had but a day's respite from the time that Jehu wrote his letter till the time that all he required should be done.

*{now the Kings sons being seventy persons, were with the great men of the city}* I.e., great in authority, dignity, honor, command, prudence, wealth, and other like excellencies. See ch. 5. 1. & 4. 8.

*{which brought them up}* Heb. *made them great.* Education is a means of making great, Isa. 1. 2. See v. 1.

**V. 7. ***{And it came to pass when the letter came to them}* So soon as they had read it.

*{that they took the Kings sons}* They apprehended them.

*{and slew seventy persons}* Too much care for their own safety made them cast off all care of their charge.

*{and put their heads in baskets, and sent him them to Jezreel}* Though others carried this doleful burden, yet they also went along therewith, as Jehu had required, v. 6.

**V. 8. ***{And there came a messenger and told him, saying}* It seems they were afraid at first to come themselves into his presence, therefore they sent a messenger in to him to give him notice of their coming.

*{They have brought the heads of the Kings sons}* See v. 7.

*{and he said, Lay ye them in two heaps}* A lamentable spectacle, to see so many princes' heads lying together. One heap might be laid on one side of the entrance, and the other on the other side.

*{at the entering in of the gate}* The gate of the city, the place of justice, where people used to assemble, Deut. 22. 15. Ruth 4. 1. They were here laid, that there might be the more eyewitnesses of God's just revenge upon the idolatrous stock of Ahab. And this was a fit place for Jehu to clear his justice therein, and to show the ground for which there were so many slain. See v. 10.

*{until the morning}* It was now evening when they came, and probably the gates of the city were shut: and therefore he would do nothing till the next day appeared, that there might be the more witnesses.

**V. 9. ***{And it came to pass in the morning}* He was careful betimes to make known his mind.

*{that he went out}* From his chamber and house where he lay.

*{and stood}* That he might be the better seen and heard.

*{and said to all the people}* Both those that came from Samaria, and others also. For certainly so strange a matter could not but draw many people together.

*{Ye be righteous}* Though some apply this to those that came from Samaria, and slew those seventy persons, as if Jehu had cleared them of all guilt, yet the last clause in this verse, *but who slew all these?* shows that he here speaks to those that were only spectators, and had no hand in the destruction of those whom he himself slew, nor of those whom the men of Samaria slew. And them he acquits, that they might the more attentively hearken to the apology he made for the blood that was shed.

*{behold, I conspired against my master}* He sets forth his executing of God's vengeance by that phrase which might seem to imply treason, (see 1 King. 16. 9.) that his defense of himself might make the deeper impression.

*{and slew him}* See ch. 9. 24.

*{but who slew all these?}* This he says to show that they who slew those seventy persons might seem to have their hands as deep in blood as he; yet the apology following extends itself to them as well as to himself.

**V. 10. ***{Know now}* By this evidence he would convince them of God's truth in accomplishing his threatenings.

*{that there shall fall unto the earth}* This phrase is used of such things as perish, 1 King. 1. 52. 2 Sam. 3. 19.

*{nothing of the word of the Lord}* He sets it down thus generally, by reason of that great execution of vengeance which was made.

*{which the Lord spake concerning the house of Ahab}* That which is here in particular spoken of the house of Ahab may be applied to all other threatenings of the Lord against any other.

*{for the Lord hath done that which he spake}* He has caused to be accomplished that which he threatened to the very uttermost.

*{by his servant Elijah}* Heb. *by the hand of, etc.* 1 King. 8. 56. Elijah's prophecy was public, 1 King. 21. 21. and known almost to all: (See ch. 9. 25.) therefore is his name expressed, rather than the young prophet's who in private gave the charge to Jehu, ch. 9. 6, 7.

**V. 11. ***{So Jehu slew all that remained to the house of Ahab in Jezreel}* In Jezreel had Ahab a palace, 1 King. 21. 1. and there were many of his kindred, familiar friends, and others that might be accounted of his *house.*

*{and all his great men}* All such as Ahab had raised and put in great places, or made rich. See v. 6. Ruth 2. 1. We call such the king's creatures.

*{and his kinsfolk}* Or, *acquaintance.* Such as were by kindred, alliance, familiarity or otherwise linked to him.

*{and his Priests}* His peculiar chaplains, or such as he had preferred to their places. We read of *four hundred Prophets of the Groves, which ate at Jezebels table*, 1 King. 18. 19. These priests here mentioned might be such as ate at Ahab's table, such as were maintained by him, and thereupon called his *priests.*

*{until he left him none remaining}* In this vengeance of Ahab's house his zeal was as great as could be expected.

**V. 12. ***{And he arose, and departed and came to Samaria}* Now that he had tried the fidelity of those that dwelt at Samaria unto him, he readily comes into that great city, which was the metropolis or chief city of the kingdom of Israel.

*{and as he was at the shearing-house}* Heb. *house of shepherds binding sheep.* Where they were wont to bind and shear their sheep. The Hebrew word implying only *binding*, it may be applied to a slaughterhouse also.

*{in the way}* Namely, as he was going to Samaria.

**V. 13. ***{Jehu met}* Heb. *found.* Though he sought not for them, yet when he espied them his mind was set to destroy them; therefore he may be said to *find* them in his desire, though not in his endeavor.

*{with the brethren of Ahaziah king of Judah}* 2 Chron. 22. 8. these are called *the Princes of Judah, and the sons of the brethren of Ahaziah, that ministred to Ahaziah.* Ahaziah was Joram's youngest son, and had never a brother left; For the Arabians had taken away all the other sons of Joram, 2 Chron. 21. 17. Therefore the word *brethren* must here be taken in a large sense, for his brethren's sons, or other of his kindred. Lot was Abraham's *brother's son*, Gen. 12. 5. yet called his *brother*, Gen. 13. 8. The title *brethren* is oft taken for any kinsfolks. These are also styled *the Princes of Judah*, because places of dignity and government were committed unto them. And they are said *to minister to Ahaziah*, because they were officers of his court.

*{and said, Who are ye?}* He would know who they were before he offered any violence unto them, to clear his justice in what he did.

*{And they answered, We are the brethren of Ahaziah}* They declare the truth; for they feared no evil to follow thereupon.

*{and we go down to salute}* Heb. *to the peace of, etc.* To wish peace and prosperity to them. Of this phrase see ch. 4. 26.

*{the children of the King and the children of the Queen}* I.e., of Joram king of Israel, and Athaliah his wife. Yet Ahab also and Jezebel may be here meant: for the title queen was continued to Jezebel after her husband's death. It seems they knew nothing of Joram's and Jezebel's and Ahaziah's death, and of the vengeance that Jehu had executed on Ahab's stock; and that made them so readily declare not only who they were, but also why they came, which was more than Jehu asked them.

**V. 14. ***{And he said, Take them alive}* This charge he gave, because he had them in his power, so as they could neither defend themselves nor flee away; and the rather, that the cause of slaying them might be made known to them, which was their affinity with the house of Ahab.

*{And they took them alive}* Jehu had now such a power, as all of all sorts, whether on his own side or on the other, were ready to obey him. See v. 7.

*{and slew them}* The reason of this act is thus rendered 2 Chron. 22. 8. *When Jehu was executing judgment upon the house of Ahab, he slew them:* So as it was a part of his commission, ch. 9. 8. to slay these also.

*{at the pit of the shearing-house}* See v. 12.

*{even two and forty men; neither left he any of them}* In this, his zeal extended itself to the uttermost.

**V. 15. ***{And when he was departed thence}* Namely, from that shearing-house.

*{he lighted on}* Heb. *found.* See v. 13.

*{Jehonadab the son of Rechab}* This Rechab was a Kenite 1 Chr. 2. 55. descended from Jethro Moses' father-in-law, Exod. 2. 21. & 18. 1, etc. Judg. 1. 16. & 4. 11. The Kenites were a pious generation, and joined themselves to the people of God. Jehonadab was a prudent man, and foreseeing the future estate of the Jews, gave good directions to his posterity, Jer. 35. 5, etc. His father was also a man of great note and name; and therefore for honor's sake, Jehonadab is here styled *the son of Rechab*, and their posterity are called *Rechabites*, Jer. 35. 2.

*{coming to meet him}* Questionless Jehonadab knew that Jehu was God's minister to execute vengeance on Ahab's house, and therefore might purposely come to meet him.

*{and he saluted him}* Heb. *blessed him.* Or, *wished peace and prosperity to him.* Jehu blessed Jehonadab; for he was now the greater man, Heb. 7. 7. This was a testimony of Jehu's high esteem of Jehonadab, and of his good respect to him.

*{and said to him, Is thine heart right}* He means hereby an upright and entire heart to him.

*{as my heart is with thy heart}* Do you in truth affect me as I do you? and do you approve my course as I do yours?

*{And Jehonadab answered, It is}* Heb. *It is, and it is.* This doubling of his affirmation adds great emphasis. See 1 King. 8. 13. Hereby he assures him that he did approve him and bear a good respect to him.

*{If it be, give me thine hand}* This he said for a further pledge of his entire affection to him.

*{And he gave him his hand}* He was willing to give him all the assurance he required.

*{and he took him up to him into the chariot}* Hereby Jehu testified his high esteem of Jehonadab, whom he took into his chariot for ease and honor's sake.

**V. 16. ***{And he said, Come with me}* Jehu sought countenance by the company of Jehonadab, whom all the people had in high account for his piety and prudence.

*{and see my zeal for the Lord}* The Lord by his prophet had commanded him to do what he did, ch. 9. 6, 7. and therefore he might call it *zeal for the Lord.* See 1 King. 19. 10.

*{so they made him ride in his chariot}* Jehu's attendants moved him to accept Jehu's honorable offer, and to accompany him; and withal, helped him into the chariot.

**V. 17. ***{And when he came to Samaria}* As he intended and began his journey thereunto, v. 12.

*{he slew all that remained unto Ahab}* Seventy of Ahab's posterity were slain before, v. 7. yet there remained sundry others that were allied to him, or some other way appertained unto him: These also he slew.

*{in Samaria}* What he had done before in Jezreel, v. 11. that he now does in Samaria.

*{till he had destroyed him}* Under this relative *him*, which has reference to Ahab, all that any way might be accounted his are comprised.

*{according to the saying of the Lord which he spake to Elijah}* 1 Kin. 21. 21, etc. See 1 King. 13. 5.

**V. 18. ***{And Jehu gathered all the people together}* Jehu was willing to acquaint all the people with his proceedings, that he might the more insinuate himself into them.

*{and said unto them, Ahab served Baal a little}* Ahab served Baal more than any had done before him, 1 King. 16. 31, 32. but Jehu would here make the people believe that Ahab's practice in this kind was not comparable to that he intended.

*{but Jehu shall serve him much}* This he pretended, the rather to move all Baal's priests to assemble to him, and to take away all fear of hurt or damage to the worshipers of Baal. It seems the people were much addicted to that idolatry, in that Jehu makes this outward profession before them all.

**V. 19. ***{Now therefore call unto me all the Prophets of Baal}* Which pretend to be inspired by him.

*{all his servants}* Which profess him to be their lord.

*{and all his Priests}* Which offer sacrifice to him.

*{let none be wanting}* As he destroyed all that belonged unto Ahab, v. 11, 17. so he would do the like to all that any way subjected themselves to Baal.

*{for I have a great sacrifice to do to Baal}* He had no purpose to do so, but it was a mere pretense to deceive the Baalites. Though his end herein were good, yet this means that he uses was not warrantable.

*{whosoever shall be wanting, he shall not live}* By this threatening he brings those into the snare, which otherwise might have escaped it.

*{But Jehu did it in subtilty}* It was such a subtle course as thereby he accomplished what he intended. See v. 25.

*{to the intent that he might destroy the worshippers of Baal}* This was a good end: for the law commanded that idolaters should be destroyed. See Deut. 13. 2, 5. Thus Constantius the father of Constantine the Great commanded all Christians to avoid his court; but they were no sooner departed, but he called them back again, and quite excluded all the rest.

**V. 20. ***{And Jehu said, Proclaim a solemn assembly}* Heb. *sanctify.* Or, *sanctify a set day*, as is appointed by the Lord, Deut. 16. 8. So Exod. 32. 5. Lev. 23. 36. Joel 1. 14.

*{for Baal}* For a solemn service to be performed to him.

*{And they proclaimed it}* Jehu's officers openly made known that which he pretended, far and near.

**V. 21. ***{And Jehu sent through all Israel}* He sent the aforesaid proclamation not only into every street of Samaria, but also through all the ten tribes.

*{and all the worshippers of Baal came}* They saw no cause to mistrust the truth of what Jehu pretended, and therefore came boldly.

*{so that there was not a man left that came not}* Certainly they expected at least some approbation, if not a reward for their pains. God suffered them thus to be beguiled, that they might be destroyed for their idolatry.

*{and they came into the house of Baal}* There was a special house built for this idol in Samaria, 1 King. 16. 32. a temple dedicated to Baal; as the house of the Lord, 1 King. 8. 16.

*{and the house of Baal was full from one end to another}* Or, *so full that they stood mouth to mouth.* So full as their faces or mouths were close together, and there was not a corner empty. See ch. 21. 16. Thus were the Egyptians emboldened to rush into the sea, Exod. 14. 23.

**V. 22. ***{And he said unto him that was over the vestry}* That had the charge of keeping all such things as appertained to the worship of Baal.

*{Bring forth vestments for all the worshippers of Baal}* All sort of idolaters were wont to have peculiar vestments for their idolatrous service.

*{And he brought them forth vestments}* This was done that the Baalites might be the more beguiled, and think that Jehu indeed intended what he pretended.

**V. 23. ***{And Jehu went and Jehonadab the son of Rechab into the house of Baal}* They went not thither to worship that idol, but to deliver such as might upon curiosity go to see their service, or upon any other occasion be there, and yet were no worshipers of Baal.

*{and said unto the worshippers of Baal}* These he knew would be so zealous of their idol as they would suffer none but such as were of their own mind to be there present.

*{Search and look that there be here with you none of the servants of the Lord}* This title *servants of the Lord* is opposite to that other, *worshippers of Baal.* Neither of these would endure that the other part should join with them in their service. Such respect did Jehu bear to these latter as he would have none of them destroyed.

*{but the worshippers of Baal only}* These he devoted to destruction.

**V. 24. ***{And when they went in}* I.e., the worshipers of Baal.

*{to offer sacrifices and burnt offerings}* *Sacrifices* is a more general word; *burnt offerings* were a particular kind of sacrifice. See 1 Kin. 3. 15. In offering these idolaters imitated the custom of God's people.

*{Jehu appointed fourscore men without}* He appointed them to stand without at the doors of Baal's temple, to keep those that were within from flying away, and to destroy such as should offer to come out.

*{and said, If any of the men whom I have brought into your hands}* Those whom Jehu enticed to come into Baal's temple were thereby brought into the hands of those that belonged to the temple of the Lord.

*{escape}* By coming out of that house and flying away.

*{he that letteth him go, his life shall be for the life of him}* The word translated *life* signifies the *soul.* Now the life of the body consists in the union of the soul with it. His meaning is, That whosoever suffered any of the Baalites to slip away should die for it. See 1 King. 20. 39, 40.

**V. 25. ***{And it came to pass as soon as he had made an end of offering the burnt offering}* This is to be taken of Baal's chief priest, who then offered the burnt offering here mentioned. Now Jehu tarried till the offering was finished, that there might be time for other idolaters that were not then come to enter in among them.

*{that Jehu said to the guard}* To such officers as attended his person.

*{and to the Captains}* Such as had a command over soldiers. Both these were other than the fourscore men mentioned v. 24.

*{Go in, and slay them}* This was the end why Jehu at this time assembled them together. See v. 19.

*{let none come forth}* His meaning was that they who had the charge to slay these Baalites, should destroy every one of them.

*{And they smote them with the edge of the sword}* Heb. *the mouth of the sword.* For as the mouth of a ravening beast devours the prey that it takes, so by the edge of the sword many are destroyed and as it were devoured.

*{and the guard and the Captains cast them out}* They cast the dead bodies of the Baalites who were slain, out of that house where they were slain, and out of the city, to ravenous beasts and fowls, affording them no burial: which aggravated the judgment.

*{and went to the city of the house of Baal}* This is to be taken of some special city where there was a choice temple for Baal; or else it is distributively to be taken for every city where a house of Baal was. Some take this city to be Samaria itself.

**V. 26. ***{And they brought forth the images}* Heb. *statues.* I.e., representations of men or other creatures carved in wood, or cut in stone, or cast in some metal. Hereby are meant all sorts of idolatrous images. See 1 King. 14. 23.

*{out of the house of Baal}* See v. 25.

*{and burnt them}* In sign of the greater detestation of them, and that they might not be used again for idolatry.

**V. 27. ***{And they brake down the image of Baal}* That principal statue which was deemed to be Baal himself. See 1 King. 16. 31. This they broke all to pieces.

*{and brake down the house of Baal, and made it a draught-house}* I.e., a jakes; or, as we say, a privy. This also they did to testify the abominableness of the idol which there stood, and of the idolatry which was there used. See the like Ezr. 6. 11. Dan. 2. 5. & 3. 29. See also Lev. 26. 30. The idols of the Gentiles had a name given them in Hebrew which signifies *dirty* or *dungy gods*; in this respect a jakes was a fit house for them. See 1 King. 15. 12.

*{unto this day}* See 1 King. 9. 13.

**V. 28. ***{Thus Jehu destroyed Baal out of Israel}* *Israel* is here put for all the ten tribes; so as two things are here implied, 1. That Ahab, Jezebel and their posterity had caused Baal to be worshiped in all the ten tribes. 2. That Jehu's zeal against Baal was such as he caused that idolatry to be rooted out of all the tribes wheresoever it was established.

**V. 29. ***{Howbeit}* Men zealous in some things which are commendable may grievously sin in others.

*{from the sins of Jeroboam the son of Nebat who made Israel to sin}* See 1 King. 11. 26. & 14. 16.

*{Jehu departed not from after them}* See ch. 3. 3.

*{to wit, the golden calves that were in Bethel, and that were in Dan}* See 1 King. 12. 28, 29.

**V. 30. ***{And the Lord said unto Jehu}* Divers conjectures are made about the prophet that should bring this message: Some say it was Elisha; some, the young man that brought the first message about Jehu's being king, ch. 9. 4, etc. some the prophet Jonah: But that which the Holy Ghost has concealed is not too curiously to be searched after.

*{Because thou hast done well}* The thing that Jehu did is here commended, not his manner of doing it. See Hos. 1. 4.

*{in executing that which is right in mine eyes}* In destroying Baal and Ahab's stock.

*{and hast done unto the house of Ahab according to all that was in mine heart}* Which I had determined, foretold, and commanded to be done. To destroy such as God would have destroyed is an acceptable work.

*{thy children of the fourth generation}* This was accordingly accomplished: For Jehoahaz was Jehu's son, ch. 13. 1. Joash his grandchild, ch. 13. 9. Jeroboam the third from Jehu, ch. 14. 16. Zachariah the fourth, ch. 14. 29.

*{shall sit on the throne of Israel}* As kings shall reign over Israel.

**V. 31. ***{But Jehu took no heed}* Heb. *observed not.* Or, *regarded not.*

*{to walk in the law}* See 1 King. 2. 3.

*{of the Lord God of Israel}* See 1 King. 8. 15. Though Israel had forsaken the Lord, and worshiped idols, yet the Lord had not clean cast off Israel.

*{with all his heart}* See 1 King. 2. 4. They who in some things do what good God commands, but transgress in other things, do not serve him with all their heart.

*{for he departed not, &c.}* See 1 King. 16. 19.

**V. 32. ***{In those days}* Even while Jehu lived.

*{the Lord began to cut Israel short}* Heb. *to cut off the ends of Israel.* This was done by suffering Hazael to take away some of their coasts, as is expressed in the words following.

*{and Hazael smote them in all the coasts of Israel}* Here begins that prophecy to be accomplished which was foretold by Elisha, ch. 8. 12.

**V. 33. ***{From Jordan Eastward}* Heb. *toward the rising of the sun.* The particulars here intended are specified in the next words. He means that side of Jordan which Moses conquered and took from Sihon and Og, Num. 21. 24, 25. That country lay eastward from Canaan.

*{all the land of Gilead}* All that country which by Moses was distributed to Reuben, Gad, and half Manasseh, Deut. 3. 12, 13. In this land was a mountain, Gen. 31. 21. on which a city was built, Josh. 17. 1. and the mountain, city and country were all called *Gilead.* Of the land of Gilead there were many cities.

*{the Gadites, and the Reubenites, and the Manassites}* All these inhabited the land of Gilead taken in the largest sense.

*{from Aroer}* A city in the furthest coast of Gad, Deut. 2. 36. A native or inhabitant thereof is called an *Aroerite*, 1 Chron. 11. 44.

*{which is by the river Arnon}* Num. 21. 13, 24. Deut. 2. 24.

*{even Gilead and Bashan}* Or, *even to Gilead and Bashan.* *Gilead* here signifies a city. Gilead and Bashan were two fair cities in Manasseh, 1 Chro. 5. 11. Josh. 17. 1. The forementioned particulars show that Hazael had cut Israel short indeed, as v. 32. and done much mischief to them.

**V. 34. ***{Now the rest of the acts of Jehu, &c.}* See 1 King. 14. 19.

*{and all his might}* See 1 King. 15. 23.

*{are they not written, &c.}* See 1 King. 16. 27.

**V. 35. ***{And Jehu slept with his fathers, &c.}* See 1 King. 2. 10.

*{and Jehoahaz, &c.}* This son of Jehu had a right to the crown by God's appointment, v. 30. See 1 King. 16. 28.

**V. 36. ***{And the time}* Heb. *the days were.* See 1 King. 11. 42.

*{that Jehu reigned over Israel in Samaria}* See 1 King. 16. 26.

*{was twenty and eight years}* This was the longest time that any of the kings of Israel hitherto had reigned; but Jeroboam the third from him reigned longer, ch. 14. 23.

## CHAPTER 11

**Verse 1. ***{And when Athaliah the mother of Ahaziah}* 2 Chron. 22. 10. This Athaliah was Ahab's daughter, and married to Jehoram the son of Jehoshaphat, by whom she had Ahaziah here mentioned. See ch. 8. 18, 26.

*{saw that her son was dead}* Slain by Jehu, ch. 9. 27.

*{she arose}* She usurped the crown and advanced herself to the throne, and was queen. Of this word *arose* see 1 King. 14. 2, 4. It is probable that when Ahaziah went with Joram to the war, ch. 8. 28. that he left his mother to govern in his absence; who thereupon had the greater advantage to usurp the kingdom after her son's death.

*{and destroyed all the seed royal}* Heb. *seed of the kingdom.* Namely such as were of the blood royal descending from David. Many of that royal offspring were slain by Jehoram, 2 Chron. 21. 4. Others were carried away by the Philistines and Arabians, 2 Chron. 21. 16, 17. others were slain by Jehu, ch. 10. 14. The remainder Athaliah here destroys; so as she thought there was none left to lay claim to the crown. Questionless some of this seed royal came out of her own womb, but idolatry and ambition made her cast off all humanity and natural affection. See the like Judg. 9. 5.

**V. 2. ***{But Jehosheba}* She is called *Jehoshabeath* 2 Chron. 22. 11. It is there said that *she was the wife of Jehojada the Priest.* Priests were then in such high account as kings married their daughters to them.

*{the daughter of king Joram}* It is not likely that she was Joram's daughter by Athaliah: For then Jehojada the priest, being so good a man, would not have married her, being the daughter of such an idolatress. It is therefore supposed that king Joram had her by another wife.

*{sister of Ahaziah}* By the father.

*{took Joash the son of Ahaziah}* He is called *Jehoash* ch. 12. 1. He was at that time but a year old at most. See v. 21.

*{and stole him from among the kings sons which were slain}* This phrase implies that this child was laid among the dead corpses of his brethren. This is supposed to have been done by his nurse, and afterwards made known to Jehosheba, who privily took him away. This nurse having notice of the queen's intent to destroy all the royal seed, kept herself and the child from being taken, till Jehosheba took them to herself. She did herein as God himself afterwards did, Jer. 36. 26. and Joseph, Mat. 2. 14.

*{and they hid him}* Under this word *they* Jehosheba and her husband Jehojada are comprised: for with his advice and assistance she did what she did.

*{even him and his nurse}* Jehosheba finding the child's nurse so careful of him and faithful to him, hid her with the child that she might the better tend him.

*{in the bedchamber}* The priests had chambers built on the sides of the temple, 1 King. 6. 8. Jer. 35. 2. Ezek. 40. 45, 46. Some of these were lodging-chambers wherein the priests had their beds; others were to hold and preserve such things as were useful for the services of the temple. In one of these which belonged to Jehojada was the child and his nurse hid.

*{from Athaliah}* Who would have slain him, if she could have known where to have found him.

*{so that he was not slain}* With his brethren and kindred.

**V. 3. ***{And he was with her}* Joash, and his nurse.

*{hid in the house of the Lord}* Chambers and courts appertaining to the temple were styled *the house of the Lord.* See 1 King. 6. 26.

*{six years}* It was so long before Jehojada could find a fair opportunity to settle the king on his throne. See v. 4.

*{and Athaliah did reign over the land}* The land of Judah is here meant, over which that wicked woman reigned by force and usurpation.

**V. 4. ***{And the seventh year}* Jehojada so long concealed the king, not thinking himself sufficiently prepared to defend him against the queen's power: It cannot be but that all those six years he cast all the ways he could how to suppress the usurping queen, and set the king upon his throne: And in that time he questionless communicated his counsels to some faithful friends. But now he thought it no wisdom to suffer that usurper to continue any longer, or her idolatry to get more strength and infect the more: Besides, it might be prejudicial to the young king to be kept any longer in a private and retired course of life. It was now high time he should be brought forth and made known, that a royal spirit might be the better put into him, and he might be educated in a kingly manner. And further, the people might be by this time very sensible of the queen's tyrannical government, and of her eagerness to advance Baal.

*{Jehoiada}* This was the high priest at that time, and of the house of Zadok, whom Solomon put in the room of Abiathar, 1 King. 1. 35. He was a very good and prudent man, a supporter of church and state, and so continued till he was a hundred and thirty years old, 2 Chron. 24. 15.

*{sent and fet the Rulers over hundreds, with the Captains and the guard}* These were Levites; for they were brought into the house of the Lord. They are named 2 Chr. 23. 1. The Levites had their companies and troops, and captains and commanders over them, as well as others of Israel. See 1 Chron. 9. 10, etc. They might be before in their several cities, from whence Jehojada sent for them.

*{and brought them to him into the house of the Lord}* There he might more securely communicate his counsel with them.

*{and made a covenant with them}* This covenant might be to depose Athaliah, to set Joash on the throne, to put down idolatry, and to establish true religion. When good governors undertook to reform religion they used to begin with a covenant, to bind themselves and their subjects more steadfastly thereto. See 2 Chr. 15. 12. & 29. 10. & 34. 31. Ezr. 10. 3. Neh. 9. 38.

*{and took an oath of them}* Of secrecy and fidelity. Hereby he would bind them the more firmly to their covenant, 2 Chron. 15. 14. Neh. 5. 12. & 10. 29.

*{in the house of the Lord}* Where priests and Levites did their services, and whither none else might come, 2 Chr. 23. 6.

*{and shewed them the King’s son}* That by sight of him they might have the surer evidence of the reality of Jehojada's intentions, and be the more quickened up to do what possibly they could to bring him to the possession of his right.

**V. 5. ***{And he commanded them, saying}* In so weighty a cause the good priest takes upon him the authority of a commander: for he was at this time the king’s protector.

*{This is the thing that ye shall do}* This he premises to make them the more attentive to his direction.

*{A third part of you}* The priests and Levites were so divided as they might serve by course, 1 Chr. 24.1, &c. Luke 1.8,9. While one part served in the house of the Lord, two parts rested and went unto their own houses. But at this time they were all assembled together, and according to the said division into three parts they were assigned to three several charges.

*{that enter in on the Sabbath}* To do service, wherein they continued from Sabbath to Sabbath so long as their course lasted, 1 Chr. 9.25. This shows that these were all Levites.

*{shall even be keepers of the watch}* Shall carefully and diligently watch.

*{of the Kings house}* Of the north gate, that leads out of the temple into the royal palace. Here a strong watch was set, lest any should issue out of the palace to destroy the king. Some understand by the king’s house the chamber where the king now was kept.

**V. 6. ***{And a third part shall be at the gate of Shur}* This was the east gate, called *the gate of the foundation*, 2 Chr. 23.5. By it were seats of judgment, Jer. 26.10. It is called *the high gate*, ch. 15.35, by reason of the fairness and height of it; and *the new gate*, Jer. 36.10, after Jotham had new built it, 2 Chr. 27.3.

*{and a third part at the gate behind the guard}* At the backside of those that kept the watch, ver. 5. This was the fourth gate.

*{so shall ye keep the watch of the house}* You shall thus keep the house safe and secure.

*{that it be not broken down}* Or, *from breaking down*. Or, that none breaking in do any violence to the king, or to any other person or thing in the house.

**V. 7. ***{And two parts}* Or, *companies*. Heb. *hands*. As the two hands of the body protect it from danger, so these two hands of men were to protect the king.

*{of all you that go forth on the Sabbath}* i.e. They who had finished their course, and so had liberty to go to their own habitations, were also now to abide, and to guard the king. See v. 15. Or, if there were but two parts, then it may be thus turned, *And two parts, even all you that go forth, &c.* 2 Chr. 23.8.

*{even they shall keep the watch of the house of the Lord}* See ver. 6.

*{about the King}* There where the king was placed.

**V. 8. ***{And ye shall compass the King round about}* For if any of Athaliah’s part should with violence and by force thrust in, they would be sure first to set upon the king; therefore he wisely takes care to have him the most surely guarded.

*{every man with his weapons in his hand}* This was an extraordinary case, and therefore an unusual course is taken. For Levites were not wont to stand armed with warlike weapons.

*{and he that cometh within the ranges}* Within those ranks and orders of men that were set to defend the king.

*{let him be slain}* Though man’s blood were not ordinarily to be shed in the house of God, (for a person guilty of blood, and laying hold on the altar, was to be taken from it and slain without, Exo. 21.14) yet in extraordinary cases it was not unlawful. See 1 King. 2.31.

*{and be ye with the King}* Some were to oppose against all violence that should be offered; others, to guard the king.

*{as he goeth out and as he cometh in}* Continually; whether he come into the temple and there abide, or go out of it to his own house.

**V. 9. ***{And the Captains over the hundreds}* Mentioned v. 4.

*{did according to all things that Jehoiada the Priest commanded}* For God stirred them up to help Jehojada in that great work. They were therefore very willing to be ordered by him.

*{and they took every man}* Every captain or commander.

*{his men that were come in on the Sabbath}* To serve their course in the house of the Lord.

*{with them that should go out on the Sabbath}* That had finished their courses, and were by order to have gone to their own houses, had not this extraordinary case fallen out.

*{and came to Jehoiada the Priest}* To know his pleasure, and what service he would require of them.

**V. 10. ***{And to the Captains over hundreds}* For themselves and for those men that were under them.

*{did the Priest give King Davids spears and shields}* Such weapons as were reserved for trophies and monuments of David’s victories. See 1 Sam. 21.9. 2 Sam. 8.7. Or they might be such as David had dedicated to remain for some special and extraordinary use, as occasion should require.

*{that were in the Temple}* These weapons of war may be reckoned in the number of the things dedicated by David, and brought into the temple by Solomon, 1 King. 7.51.

**V. 11. ***{And the guard stood}* The guard of Levites mentioned ver. 14.

*{every man with his weapons in his hand}* Such as the priest had given them, v. 10, and others that they might have from other places.

*{round about the King}* According to the direction v. 7.

*{from the right corner}* Heb. *shoulder*. The shoulder sticks out of the body, and knits it together, and the corner of an edifice is of such use to a building as the shoulder is to the body.

*{of the Temple}* Of the great court belonging to the temple. See v. 4.

*{to the left corner of the Temple}* i.e. From north to south.

*{along by the altar and the Temple}* i.e. The great brass altar for burnt offerings. This stood at the east gate of the priests’ court. See 1 King. 3.36. 2 Chron. 24.21. And the great edifice properly called the temple stood west against it.

**V. 12. ***{And he brought forth the Kings son}* Jehojada having prepared all things for the safety of the king, brings him out of that private place where he had been kept secret for many years, to show him to all the people, that they might see him, and acknowledge him for their king. He here styles him *the king’s son*, to show the title he had to the crown, and the warrant that he and all that took his part had to engage themselves so far as they did for him. So long as he lived, Athaliah could by no show of right be taken for a queen; but all that took part with her were traitors, and not they that sought to put her down.

*{and put the crown upon him}* A crown is proper to a king, and his true title is hereby testified.

*{and gave him the testimony}* i.e. A book wherein the law of God was registered, and wherein was testified what God required of his people, and what they might expect from him; and in such respects it was called *the testimony*, or *the covenant*. See 1 King. 3.15. Exod. 25.16. The original was in the ark, 1 King. 8.9. See also ch. 22.8. There might be a special copy of this testimony remaining with the priest and he might put that into the king’s hand. See Deut. 17.18.

*{and they}* Jehojada, his sons, and others that took part with them, 2 Chro. 23.11.

*{made him King}* By using the solemnities that appertain to a king, the chief whereof are here mentioned.

*{and anointed him}* This also was a rite used about kings at their inauguration. Yet we do not read of all kings that they were anointed. For he that immediately succeeded as heir apparent to his father, his title being no ways questioned, sat upon the royal throne without any special anointing; but such as were immediately chosen of God, as Saul, 1 Sam. 10.1. David, 1 Sam. 16.13. Jehu, ch. 9.6, were anointed: so also they who were preferred before their elder brethren, as Solomon, 1 King. 1.34, and they who were made kings in troublesome times, when the land was in great fear and danger, the former king being slain, as Jehoahaz, ch. 23.30, and they who had their right interrupted by an usurper, as Jehoash in this place. Here are three special rites used to this king at the settling of him on his throne: One was, putting a crown upon his head, which was of gold curiously wrought and set with precious stones. See 2 Sam. 12.36. Another was putting the testimony into his hand, to show what was to be his warrant and direction for what he should do. The third was, anointing him, to give evidence to his true title, and to make him depend on God’s protection and blessing.

*{and they clapped their hands}* This was a visible expression of great joy, Psal. 47.1. Ezek. 25.6.

*{and said, God save the King}* Heb. *Let the king live*. See 1 King. 1.25.

**V. 13. ***{And when Athaliah heard}* The palace was not far from the temple, so as she might easily hear such acclamations as were thereabouts.

*{the noise of the guard}* Of such as in the great court stood about the king, v. 11.

*{and of the people}* The shoutings and rejoicings of such as were in other courts, or going up and down, to and from thence.

*{she came to the people}* To the multitude that were in and about those courts.

*{into the Temple of the Lord}* She violently broke through the ranges mentioned v. 8, and came into the court where the king and Levites that guarded him were, which is here called *the temple*. See 1 King. 6.36.

**V. 14. ***{And when she looked, behold, the King stood by a pillar}* There that brazen scaffold which Solomon had made, and whereon he stood when he dedicated the temple, 2 Chron. 6.13, was set; and thereon was this king, with the crown on his head, now placed, so as looking up she might soon behold him.

*{as the manner was}* This has relation to the king’s standing on high. So did other kings before and after, 2 Chron. 6.13. & 34.31. ch. 23.3.

*{and the Princes}* In this extraordinary case the princes of the land (if these were secular princes) might come into this court; or otherwise they might stand about the king, though they were in the other court.

*{and the trumpeters by the King}* Levites used to be trumpeters, 1 Chron. 16.42, as well as the priests, 2 Chron. 5.12. But to the priests appertained it most properly to sound with trumpets. See 2 Chro. 13.12.

*{and all the people of the land rejoiced}* Being weary of an usurper’s reign they were much affected at the sight of a king descended from David, and having an undoubted title.

*{and blew with trumpets}* An accustomed rite at all great solemnities, especially at a king’s inauguration. See 1 King. 1.39.

*{and Athaliah rent her clothes}* See ch. 5.7. This was a sign as of much passion, so also of much scorn and disdain.

*{and cried Treason, treason}* Having usurped the crown, she calls the setting of it upon the right king treason. But the treason was on her part: her usurpation was the treason.

**V. 15. ***{But Jehoiada the priest commanded the captains of the hundreds, the officers of the host}* See v. 5.

*{and said unto them, Have her forth without the ranges}* The ranges mentioned v. 8.

*{and him that followeth her, kill with the sword}* i.e. Such as follow her to defend her, and maintain her title.

*{For the priest had said, Let her not be slain in the house of the Lord}* See 1 King. 2.31.

**V. 16. ***{And they laid hands on her}* To carry her out of the place where she was. Or, they made a way for her.

*{and she went by the way by the which the horses came into the kings house}* This was a beaten way betwixt the temple and the palace, and the openest and readiest way.

*{and there was she slain}* In that common road, and it may be among the horses. Thus as in sin she was like her mother, so was she in punishment, ch. 9.33. It is very observable that none appeared to defend her: but the Lord had alienated the hearts of the people; and they were now weary of her government, and glad of a change.

**V. 17. ***{And Jehoiada made a covenant between the Lord and the king}* That the king should serve the Lord, and maintain his worship, and root out idolatry. This he did to bind him more solemnly to his duty, and to assure him that God would not forsake him.

*{and the people}* He knew the king’s endeavor would be to little purpose unless the people joined with him, therefore he brings them also into covenant.

*{that they should be the Lords people}* That they should fear and serve the Lord, and every way carry themselves as becomes the people of God.

*{between the king also and the people}* It was a civil covenant that was made betwixt these, namely, that the king should well govern them, and they obey him in the Lord.

**V. 18. ***{And all the people of the land went into the house of Baal}* By this it appears that Athaliah had established that grand idolatry of Ahab’s house in Judah which Jehu cast out of Israel.

*{and brake it down, his altars, and his images brake they in pieces throughly}* See ch. 10.27. Here they show their faithfulness in the speedy execution of what they had covenanted, and their zeal in the manner of doing it.

*{and slew Mattan the priest of Baal before the altars}* This they did either because that priest fled thither for refuge, as 1 King. 2.28, or to manifest the greater detestation of that idolatry, as ch. 23.16.

*{and the priest}* Jehojada.

*{appointed officers}* Heb. *offices*. The offices were before appointed by David, but Jehojada set apart such as should execute those offices.

*{over the house of the Lord}* To see the worship of God duly performed, and the services of the temple well done.

**V. 19. ***{And he took the rulers over hundreds, &c.}* See v. 4.

*{and all the people of the land}* Such as were there present. This he did for the greater safety and honor of the king.

*{and they brought down the king from the house of the Lord}* After all the rites of his coronation were finished, Athaliah slain, and the foresaid covenants made.

*{and came by the way of the gate of the guard}* That gate which was guarded for the better security of the king. It is called *the high gate*, 2 Chro. 23.20.

*{to the kings house}* That palace which Solomon built, 1 King. 7.1,8, and wherein Athaliah had lately dwelt.

*{and he sat on the throne of the kings}* Openly, as kings were wont; and this to show his indubitate right, 2 Sam. 3.10. 1 King. 1.46. & 2.12. Herein God performed his promise, 1 King. 11.36.

**V. 20. ***{And all the people of the land rejoiced}* See ver. 12,14.

*{and the city was in quiet}* For all the people took Jehoash for their king, and none rose up against him.

*{and they slew Athaliah, &c.}* See v. 16.

**V. 21. ***{Seven years old was Jehoash when he began to reign}* He was about one year old when Jehosheba took him into her custody, and she hid him six years, v. 3.

## CHAPTER 12

**Vers. 1. ***{In the seventh year of Jehu}* 2 Chr. 24.1. When Jehu took upon him the kingdom, he slew Ahaziah the father of Jehoash: and six years complete after that, Athaliah reigned, ch. 11.3. So as this must needs be the seventh year of Jehu.

*{Jehoash began to reign}* See ch. 11.12.

*{and forty years reigned he}* Most of these were in the days of Jehojada, and so it was a prosperous time to Judah.

*{in Jerusalem}* See 1 King. 11.43.

*{and his mothers name was Zibiah}* Why mothers’ names of kings are mentioned, see 1 King. 15.10.

*{of Beersheba}* See 1 King. 19.3. This being a city of Judah, this woman was no alien, but born within the church.

**V. 2. ***{And Jehoash did that which was right}* In the matter of such external acts as he did, but not in the uprightness of his heart. For he was a notorious hypocrite, as Saul, 1 Sam. 10.9. Rehoboam, 2 Chr. 17. Abijah, 2 Chr. 13.4. Amaziah, 2 Chro. 25.2, and sundry others.

*{in the sight of the Lord}* As in the presence of the Lord; or so as the Lord approved it; not in regard of his manner of doing, but in the regard of the things done. So ch. 14.3.

*{all his days wherein Jehoiada the priest instructed him}* Jehojada was a very good man, and very careful of the good of church and state, and gave the king good counsel for the well governing of both; and because he was the means of preserving the king’s life and settling him on the throne, he could not but hearken to his counsel; the rather because Jehojada was much honored of all. But after his death the king revolted from the Lord, 2 Chron. 24.17,18.

**V. 3. ***{But the high places were not taken away}* See 1 King. 15.14. As good kings suffered high places to remain, so did also this good high priest Jehojada.

*{the people}* This seems to be the reason why they were tolerated; because the people so doted on them.

*{still sacrificed}* Slew and offered up beasts to the Lord. The thing was good, but the place unwarrantable. See Deut. 12.11.

*{and burnt incense in the high places}* See 1 King. 9.25. & 3.4.

**V. 4. ***{And Jehoash said to the priests}* It is probable the king gave this direction by the advice of Jehojada.

*{All the money of the dedicated things}* Or, *holy things*. Heb. *holinesses*. See 1 King. 7.51.

*{that is brought into the house of the Lord}* Being given towards the Lord’s service. This is exemplified in three kinds of treasures that appertained to the house of God.

*{even the money of every one that passeth the account}* Which every one enrolled among the Israelites from twenty years old and upward was enjoined to give, Exod. 30.12, &c. This is called *the collection of Moses*, 2 Chr. 24.6,9. Because Moses first appointed it. This was the first kind of treasure.

*{the money that every man is set at}* Heb. *the money of the souls of his estimation*. Which the priest was to value according to the law, Lev. 27.2, &c. This was the second kind.

*{and all the money that cometh into any mans heart}* Heb. *ascendeth upon the heart of a man*. Which a man freely purposes; or, which arises from his own voluntary disposition. See Exod. 35.5, &c. This was the third.

*{to bring into the house of the Lord}* To be used about such things as appertain to the temple, whether for repair thereof, or making holy vessels, or any other divine service.

**V. 5. ***{Let the priests take it to them}* They were not only to take what was brought, but were also to go up and down to the several cities to gather it, 2 Chro. 24.5.

*{every man of his acquaintance}* Whom he knew to be liable to tax or willing to contribute, 1 Chro. 29.9,14. The Levites had their cities in every of the tribes, and thereby came to have acquaintance throughout all Israel.

*{and let them repair the breaches of the house}* There were questionless many breaches by reason of former neglect of repairing, but especially by reason of the violence of Athaliah and her children, 2 Chro. 24.7.

*{wheresoever any breach shall be found}* His desire was the temple should be thoroughly repaired. One reason might be, because himself in his childhood had been brought up in it, and preserved from great danger.

**V. 6. ***{But it was so, that in the three and twentieth year}* Heb. *in the twentieth year and third year*. Jehoash being but young when he began to reign, it is no great wonder the repairing of the house of the Lord was so long put off.

*{of king Jehoash}* Of his reign, not his age: for at that time he was about thirty years old.

*{the priests had not repaired the breaches of the house}* They were neither diligent in gathering what was enjoined, 2 Chr. 24.5, nor faithful in disbursing upon the temple what they had gathered.

**V. 7. ***{Then king Jehoash called for Jehoiada the priest, and the other priests, and said unto them}* By this it appears that the king had power over the priests and Levites; to take care that the temple and the things appertaining thereunto should be well ordered. See 2 Chron. 29.4,5. But yet he had not power to exercise their functions, 2 Chro. 26.16.

*{Why repair ye not the breaches of the house?}* The king blames them for their neglect of so great and needful a work.

*{now therefore receive no more money of your acquaintance}* Go no more about to the cities to gather any money of the people. See 2 Chron. 24.5. Because that which had been gathered was not employed to that use for which it was gathered, he restrains them from gathering any more.

*{but deliver it for the breaches of the house}* That which you have received deliver to be reserved for those that have the charge of repairing the temple.

**V. 8. ***{And the priests consented to receive no more money of the people}* The priests consented hereunto, because they would be freed from the care and charge of repairing of the temple.

*{neither to repair the breaches of the house}* Rather than have that charge lie upon them, they would receive no more money.

**V. 9. ***{But Jehoiada the priest took a chest}* He was not willing the repair of the temple should be wholly neglected, therefore seeing the former way of collecting money had no better success, he took this other course, and that by the king’s appointment, 2 Chro. 24.8. That which is called *the treasury* Mark 12.41 was such a chest.

*{and bored a hole in the lid of it}* That thereby such as were willing might put their benevolence into the chest.

*{and set it beside the altar}* This was the great altar for burnt offering, which stood at the entrance of that part of the temple, which was called *the priests’ court*, 2 Chro. 4.9, to which entrance the people might come, but no further, 2 Chro. 23.6. See 1 King. 6.36.

*{on the right side}* That was the south.

*{as one cometh into the house of the Lord}* This was at the east end of the temple.

*{and the priests that kept the door}* Heb. *threshold*. For the door of an house shuts over the threshold, and there stood the Levites, 2 Chro. 8.14. & 34.9.

*{put therein all the money that was brought into the house of the Lord}* The priests standing by the altar received of the people their freewill offerings, and what they received they put into the chest. Or the people might themselves put in what they pleased, and that in the sight of the priests.

**V. 10. ***{And it was so, when they saw that there was much money in the chest}* This they might easily discern, because they kept the door, v. 9.

*{that the kings scribe}* Or, *secretary*. See 1 King. 4.3. He was called, that the king and state might know what was contributed.

*{and the high priest came up}* Or, *high priest’s officer*. 2 Chron. 24.11. There were two witnesses, both of them public persons, one for the state, another for the church, that so all suspicion of fraud might be taken away.

*{and they put up in bags}* Heb. *bound up*. Having put up the money, they sealed up the bags.

*{and told the money that was found in the house of the Lord}* That so it might be justly known how much there was, and whether there were enough to buy such things as were needful, and to pay workmen.

**V. 11. ***{And they gave the money, being told, into the hands of them that did the work}* He means master workmen who had a charge over others.

*{that had the over-sight of the house of the Lord}* About the repair thereof.

*{and they laid it out}* Heb. *brought it forth*.

*{to the carpenters}* Heb. *workers on timber*. For timber is the proper matter for a carpenter’s work.

*{and builders}* Some distinguish these from carpenters, and take them to be another sort of workmen.

*{that wrought upon the house of the Lord}* Whatsoever their kind of work was, it tended to the repairing of the temple.

**V. 12. ***{And to masons}* These were such as laid hewn stones in the building.

*{and hewers of stone}* Such as cut great stones out of the quarries.

*{and to buy timber, and hewed stone}* They gave money both for materials and workmanship.

*{to repair the breaches of the house of the Lord}* See v. 5.

*{for all that was laid out}* Heb. *went forth*. The money being in the temple, that which was given to workmen went forth or was carried out of the temple.

*{for the house to repair it}* This was the end of bringing in and carrying out the money here mentioned.

**V. 13. ***{Howbeit, there were not made for the house of the Lord}* They were so careful about repairing the house with all needful reparations, as they would not lay out any of the money upon any other thing, though otherwise useful in the temple.

*{bowls of silver, snuffers, basons}* See 1 King. 7.50.

*{trumpets}* Of the use of these see Numb. 10.2, &c.

*{any vessels of gold, or vessels of silver, of the money that was brought into the house of the Lord}* Though such things were not made till the temple was fully repaired, yet afterwards they were, 2 Chron. 24.14.

**V. 14. ***{But they gave that to the work-men, and repaired therewith the house of the Lord}* The workmen bought materials, and did all the workmanship for the said money.

**V. 15. ***{Moreover, they reckoned not with the men into whose hand they delivered the money}* Master workmen are here meant. Such experience they had given of their faithfulness as they were trusted with great sums of money according to their demand; neither was there an account of every particular expense exacted of them. See ch. 22.7.

*{to be bestowed on work-men}* These were such as did the work themselves; the other were overseers of them.

*{for they dealt faithfully}* Heb. *in faithfulness*. They laid out the money to those very uses and purposes for which it was committed to their trust.

**V. 16. ***{The trespass-money}* Such as was for satisfaction of wrong done, Lev. 5.15.

*{and sin-money}* For sin offerings, Lev. 4.3.

*{was not brought into the house of the Lord}* Some brought trespass and sin offerings in their kind; these were the priests’, Lev. 7.7. Others that dwelt far off brought money, Deut. 14.25, and this also was to be disposed by the priests, and not to be put into the foresaid chest for repair of the temple.

*{it was the priests}* To buy sacrifices therewith.

**V. 17. ***{Then Hazael king of Syria}* See ch. 8.13,15. & 10.32.

*{went up, and fought against Gath}* This David had recovered from the Philistines, 1 Chron. 18.1, and the Jews held it to this time. It was one of the cities which Rehoboam fortified, 2 Chron. 11.8.

*{and took it}* This was after Jehoash his revolt, 2 Chr. 24.23.

*{and Hazael set his face to go up to Jerusalem}* He peremptorily resolved and prepared himself to do to Jerusalem as he had done to Gath. See Jer. 42.15. Luk. 9.51.

**V. 18. ***{And Jehoash king of Judah took all the hallowed things}* Heb. *holinesses*. See v. 4. ch. 18.15,16. 1 King. 15.18.

*{that Jehoshaphat}* See 1 King. 7.51.

*{and Jehoram, and Ahaziah his fathers, kings of Judah, had dedicated}* These two were idolaters, ch. 8.18,27, yet they might in the beginning of their reigns dedicate some things to the temple, either for custom sake, because all their predecessors used so to do; or in a superstitious conceit, thinking thereby to become prosperous, notwithstanding their idolatry.

*{and his own hallowed things}* What before he had dedicated to God, fear and diffidence makes him alienate from God, and bestow on an enemy.

*{and all the gold that was found in the treasures of the house of the Lord}* It is said 2 Chron. 24.7 that *Athaliahs sons bestowed upon Baalim all the dedicate things of the house of the Lord*. But it is probable that when the house of Baal was destroyed, ch. 11.18, those dedicate things were sanctified and brought again into God’s house.

*{and in the kings house}* He spared neither the Lord’s nor his own treasures to stop his enemy’s mouth, ch. 18.15.

*{and sent it to Hazael king of Syria, and he went away}* Heb. *went up*.

*{from Jerusalem}* Hazael twice invaded Judah: for here he departs away before he had entered Jerusalem, but after this he spoiled Jerusalem, 2 Chron. 24.23.

**V. 19. ***{And the rest, &c.}* See 1 King. 11.41. & 14.19,29. Many things about Jehoash his revolt and God’s judgments on him, are set down 2 Chro. 24.17, &c.

**V. 20. ***{And his servants arose}* Near bonds of relation restrain not men traitorously minded from murderous acts.

*{and made a conspiracy, and slew Joash}* Heb. *conspired a conspiracy*. The doubling of the word adds emphasis. Though God justly brought this judgment upon Jehoash, yet these instruments did it traitorously, (as Zimri 1 King. 16.9) therefore they were justly slain for this fact, ch. 14.5.

*{in the house of Millo}* Or, *Beth-Millo*. See Judg. 9.6. 1 King. 9.15.

*{which goeth down to Silla}* This word taken appellatively signifies *a beaten way*, and is here taken for a causeway betwixt the city and the temple. Some take it for a city not far from Millo.

**V. 21. ***{For Jozachar}* Called Zabad, 2 Chro. 24.26.

*{the son of Shimeath}* An Ammonitess, 2 Chro. 24.26.

*{and Jehozabad the son of Shomer}* She is called Shimrith, 2 Chron. 24.26, and was a Moabitess.

*{his servants, smote him, and he died}* See v. 20.

*{and they buried him with his fathers in the city of David}* But not among the kings, 2 Chron. 24.25. See 1 King. 2.10.

*{and Amaziah his son reigned in his stead}* Though traitors slew the father, yet the people set his son upon his throne.

## CHAPTER 13

**Vers. 1. ***{In the three and twentieth year}* Heb. *in the twentieth year, and third year*.

*{of Joash the son of Ahaziah}* See ch. 11.2.

*{king of Judah}* See ch. 11.12.

*{Jehoahaz the son of Jehu began to reign}* See ch. 10.35.

*{over Israel in Samaria}* See 1 King. 16.19.

*{and reigned seventeen years}* It is said v. 10 that Jehoash the son of Jehoahaz began to reign in the thirty and seventh year of Joash. Now betwixt the three and twentieth and seven and thirtieth there were but fourteen years; how then could Jehoahaz reign seventeen years? R. 1.
